Architecture Compliance Remediation

Architect Summary

Your guidelines describe a clean architecture (vertical-slice modules, three layers, 600/400/200 LOC limits, feature isolation). The audit shows the codebase started that way but drifted, because the enforcement the guidelines assume (pre-commit file-size check, CI gate) was never actually implemented. .husky/pre-commit only runs lint-staged; eslint.config.mjs has no max-lines rule.

Therefore the only way "full remediation to 90%+" survives is to fix the ratchet first, then chip down the backlog. We add real CI gates (Phase 0), then refactor app-by-app using a single repeatable decomposition playbook, with apps/api first because it is closest to compliant and has working reference modules (custom-fields, segments, themes, domains, conversations).

This is refactor-only. No behavior, API contract, or DB schema changes. Every step preserves the existing controller routes, service signatures, and response shapes.

FULL confidence track (user-selected). "Zero regressions" is enforced by a Phase 0.5 Behavior Lock: current behavior is captured as a green baseline on UNCHANGED code, then proven identical after each refactor. No file is refactored before its behavior is locked. Practical confidence target ~99% (no test suite can prove 0 errors mathematically; this is the strongest achievable for a refactor because we compare old vs new behavior directly).

Why This Shape

Without a CI LOC/boundary gate, every fixed file can silently regress the next week. Phase 0 makes compliance a ratchet (allowlist of current violators that can only shrink).

apps/api already has 4 fully-compliant reference modules. Refactors there are pattern-matching, not invention -> fastest path to a provable template.

A single decomposition playbook (below) is applied identically across all three apps, so the work is mechanical and reviewable in small PRs.

Current State (audit evidence)

apps/api: ~55-60%. 46 controllers >200 LOC, 47 services >600 LOC, 62% of modules have no repositories/, 44 layer files at module root, 27 fat controllers.

apps/agent-api: ~35-40%. 95/142 services hit DB directly, only 4 repository files, chat.service.ts = 3,948 LOC.

apps/web: ~35%. 194 components >400 LOC, ~1,253 cross-feature imports, ChatInput.tsx = 5,042 LOC.

flowchart TD
P0[Phase 0: Enforcement ratchet] --> P05[Phase 0.5: Behavior Lock harness]
P05 --> P1[Phase 1: apps/api]
P1 --> P2[Phase 2: apps/agent-api]
P2 --> P3[Phase 3: apps/web]
P05 -.->|"characterization gate per module"| P1

Per-refactor loop (every PR in Phases 1-3):

flowchart LR
Rec[Record characterization on UNCHANGED file] --> Base[Green baseline]
Base --> Ref[Refactor]
Ref --> Re[Re-run same suite]
Re -->|identical| Drop[Drop from LOC allowlist]
Re -->|any diff| Rev[Revert: behavior changed]

The Decomposition Playbook (unit of work, reused everywhere)

Backend file over limit:

Identify cohesive responsibility clusters in the file.

Controller >200: move business/DB logic into the service; controller keeps routing + guards + DTO validation only (pattern: custom-fields.controller.ts).

Service >600: split by sub-domain into multiple _.service.ts in the same services/ folder, OR extract data access into a new repositories/_.repository.ts.

Service with direct supabase.from(...): create/extend the module repositories/ file, move queries there, inject repo into service (pattern: custom-fields.repository.ts).

Root-level layer file (e.g. spaces.service.ts): move into controllers/|services/|repositories/, update module imports.

Update the module's \*.module.ts providers/exports, run typecheck + tests, confirm routes unchanged.

Frontend file over limit:

Component >400: extract sub-components into components/<category>/, extract logic into hooks/, keep the orchestrator as a containers/\*Container.tsx.

Cross-feature import: move the shared type/service/store into @/lib (or @/components for UI), update both sides.

Each playbook application = one small PR (one file or one module), so review stays under the guideline's 15-minute target.

Phase 0: Enforcement Ratchet (foundation - do first)

Goal: make violations impossible to add, measurable, and monotonically decreasing.

eslint.config.mjs

Add max-lines per file glob: 200 for **/\*.controller.ts, 600 for **/_.service.ts, 400 for \*\*/_.repository.ts, 400 for apps/web/src/**/\*.tsx components, 600 for **/containers/\*.tsx.

Add a cross-feature boundary rule for apps/web/src/features/\* using no-restricted-imports patterns (a feature may not import @/features/<other>), mirroring the existing no-restricted-syntax Supabase rule already present.

Extend the existing Supabase no-restricted-syntax rule to apps/api and apps/agent-api controllers (block direct supabase.from in \*_/_.controller.ts).

New scripts/arch/loc-allowlist.json + scripts/arch/check-loc.mjs

Generate a baseline allowlist of every file currently over limit (so CI is green today).

Script fails CI if: a non-allowlisted file exceeds limit, OR an allowlisted file grows. Allowlist entries can only be removed, never added (the ratchet).

.github/workflows/ci.yml

Add an "Architecture gate" job running node scripts/arch/check-loc.mjs and the new eslint rules.

.husky/pre-commit / package.json lint-staged

Add the LOC check on staged files so violations are caught before commit.

Exit criteria: CI green on current tree; any new oversized file or new cross-feature import fails CI.

Phase 0.5: Behavior Lock (FULL track - mandatory gate before any refactor)

Goal: make "works exactly as before" provable, not hoped. Build once, reuse for every refactor PR. Five layers, highest-confidence first.

Route + contract inventory snapshot (guards all controllers at once)

New apps/api/src/test/contract/route-inventory.test.ts and equivalent in agent-api: boot the Nest app via the existing createTestApp harness (test-helpers.ts), enumerate every registered route (method + path), and snapshot it. Any added/removed/renamed route fails.

HTTP contract characterization (per module, before refactoring it)

Use the supertest pattern from billing-endpoints.integration.test.ts to record request -> response (status, body shape, error shape) for every endpoint in the target module. These snapshots are the contract proof. Closes the 9/128 controller-coverage gap exactly where we cut.

Service characterization at god-file seams

Before splitting a god file (e.g. space-automation.service.ts), capture current outputs for representative inputs with mocked deps. Same inputs must yield same outputs after the split.

Real-DB integration tier (for data-access moves: service -> repository)

Mock Supabase proves logic, not SQL/RLS. For modules where query logic genuinely moves, run against a real Supabase test branch using the EXISTING gated mechanism in ground-truth-schema.test.ts (GROUND_TRUTH_DB_URL + GROUND_TRUTH_DB_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY), extending the org-scoping suite.

Substrate (DECIDED) = YC demo data cloned onto an isolated Supabase test branch (seed via scripts/seed-yc-demo), never prod. Read-only GET snapshots MAY additionally be taken against production sefy@olympus-digital.com as a real-world sanity check; write endpoints never touch prod.

Git-ref differential parity (the gold standard, deterministic)

New scripts/arch/parity-diff.mjs: boot the app at the pre-refactor git ref AND post-refactor ref against the SAME seeded YC-demo test branch, replay the recorded request set, assert byte-identical responses. Reset the branch to a snapshot between the two runs so write endpoints do not double-apply. One fixed branch + two code refs removes data drift (the reason we do NOT diff against live prod/staging).

Frontend behavior lock

Expand Playwright e2e (playwright.config.ts, existing specs in apps/web/e2e/) for the flows whose god components get decomposed (studio chat, team chat, spaces). Add Testing Library render/interaction tests on each god component BEFORE extraction.

Self-test the gate

scripts/arch/check-loc.mjs and parity-diff.mjs get unit tests (allowlist only shrinks; differ flags any response delta).

Exit criteria: route-inventory snapshot committed and green; characterization harness + parity-diff runner working; CI enforces "no refactor of a file without characterization coverage."

- Current repository-folder gaps include `agent-sync`, `artifacts`, `billing`, `chat`, `public-agent`, `project-runtime`, `task-agent`, `channel-agent`, and other lower-count modules. `conversations`, `mcp`, `sessions`, and `spaces-retrieval` are now reference compliant modules for this pass.

Phase 2 remediation taxonomy:

- Type A - folder root-level layer files into `controllers/`, `services/`, and later `repositories/` without changing imports/contracts.
- Type B - thin fat controllers and remove direct DB/client construction from controllers into services/repositories.
- Type C - move service/helper direct Supabase/RPC/storage/createClient access behind module-owned repositories/providers.
- Type D - split god services/tests/data catalogs after behavior locks, preserving public service/controller contracts.
- Type K - generated-debug/debris cleanup discovered while reading Phase 2 imports; remove only with behavior locks or when the touched file is in scope.

Broad scan during Type L Batch 150 found direct `fetch(...)` calls in artifact services/helpers outside the original Type L candidate set: `artifact-document-space-docs.service.ts`, `artifact-legacy-media-generate.service.ts`, `artifact-legacy-team-brain.service.ts`, `artifact-composio-runtime.service.ts`, `artifact-legacy-media-upload.service.ts`, `artifact-legacy-media-status.service.ts`, `artifact-legacy-state-meta.service.ts`, `artifact-skill-asset-fetcher.service.ts`, `artifact-funnels.service.ts`, `artifact-media-processing-operation-runtime.service.ts`, `artifact-visual-doc.service.ts`, `artifact-notifications.service.ts`, `artifact-legacy-media-provider.service.ts`, `artifact-project-runtime-actions.service.ts`, `artifact-legacy-session-campaign.service.ts`, `artifact-legacy-runtime-api.service.ts`, `artifact-pdf-render.service.ts`, `artifact-brain-ingestion-actions.service.ts`, `artifact-legacy-meta-api.service.ts`, and `ensure-space-view.ts`. Treat these as future provider-boundary batches with workflow-specific characterization tests before moving calls.

Batch loop: for each batch, read full target/importer/module/test context, add or reuse behavior-lock tests on unchanged code, run the focused baseline test plus `route-inventory` when routes move, apply behavior-neutral edits, remove replaced code/imports in the same change, rerun focused tests, focused ESLint, `@vibey/agent-api` typecheck when practical, LOC/direct-access scans, `git diff --check`, stale changelog scan, then update this plan/changelog/follow-up log.

Exit criteria: Type A root-level layer files removed, Type B controllers are thin and under their limits or logged as scoped follow-up, repositories/providers present for modules with owned data access, no service >600 in chat/artifacts/agent-sync/brain/billing/channel-agent/task-agent/public-agent/project-runtime unless explicitly tracked in the allowlist/follow-up log, direct-access scan outside repositories/providers is near-empty or documented false positives, and focused CI verification is green.

5.139. Phase 3 process update: frontend remediation now requires the `architecture-compliance-remediation` skill's runtime guard before additional apps/web slices. For React component, hook, store, context, effect, streaming/chat, router, or provider changes, focused tests must include mounted Testing Library coverage and render-stability checks where relevant; risky surfaces also need browser/dev-server smoke verification or an explicit reported gap. This corrects the Phase 3 verification gate so typecheck, ESLint, and pure helper tests are no longer treated as enough for frontend runtime safety.

5.140. Phase 3-E added to the plan: shared frontend surfaces need a discoverable registry plus domain-level public indexes before broadening the remaining Phase 3-A/B cleanup. The work is queued as `Type 3-E` with acceptance criteria for `documentation/frontend-shared-surfaces.md`, explicit shared `components/*/index.ts` and `lib/*/index.ts` barrels where useful, and an `AGENTS.md` pointer so humans and agents can find existing shared UI/contracts before creating duplicates or importing private feature internals.

Exit criteria: cross-feature import count near 0 (enforced by Phase 0 rule), no component >400 outside allowlist, allowlist near-empty, CI green.

Phase 4: Docs Alignment

Update project-architecture.md section on web API routes to document the actual BFF/proxy pattern (/api/proxy/[...path] -> NestJS), since the doc's per-feature /api/\* tree no longer matches reality.

Update the post-VibeyHQ monorepo tree (already partially done).

Data And Contract Map

This is a refactor program. For every step: Input/Validation/AuthZ/Storage/Output/Side-effects/Idempotency are UNCHANGED. The verification gate is "routes, request DTOs, response shapes, and tests identical before and after."

Test Plan (FULL track)

Mandatory per-PR acceptance gate (applies to EVERY refactor PR in Phases 1-3):

Characterization tests for the target file/module exist and pass on the UNCHANGED code (green baseline). If they cannot pass on unchanged code, the test is wrong - fix the test, not the code.

Refactor.

Re-run the SAME suite + node scripts/arch/parity-diff.mjs for the touched routes -> must be identical.

pnpm typecheck, pnpm lint, pnpm test, node scripts/arch/check-loc.mjs all green.

Only then remove the file from scripts/arch/loc-allowlist.json.

Tiers:

Structural: route+contract inventory snapshot (whole app).

Logic: HTTP contract characterization via supertest (per touched module).

Data: real-DB integration via `GROUND_TRUTH_DB_*` against a seeded Supabase test branch (data-access moves only).

Differential: git-ref parity diff (pre vs post ref, same branch) for highest-risk modules (billing, missions, chat, artifacts).

Frontend: Playwright e2e for decomposed flows + Testing Library tests on each god component pre-extraction.

Rollout And Verification

One module (backend) or one component/import-edge (frontend) per PR. Small, reviewable, revertible.

Behavior lock runs first on the unchanged file; the PR cannot proceed without a green baseline.

After each PR: parity diff identical -> remove the now-compliant file from scripts/arch/loc-allowlist.json; CI proves the ratchet tightened.

Track two metrics per app: allowlist length (monotonic decrease) and characterization coverage of touched seams (monotonic increase).

Rollback: each PR is an isolated refactor; revert the PR. Phase 0/0.5 gate changes are config/test-only and independently revertible.

Missing Evidence

Exact safe split boundaries inside each god file (e.g. which clusters in space-automation.service.ts): Smallest experiment = read the full target file at refactor time and map its method clusters before splitting. Risk if skipped: splitting along the wrong seam creates circular deps. (Resolved per-file during execution.)

Whether any oversized file is generated/vendored (allowlist permanently, do not refactor): Smallest experiment = grep for codegen headers when building the Phase 0 baseline.

Data substrate (DECIDED): YC demo data cloned onto an isolated Supabase test branch is the substrate for the real-DB tier + parity diff. Rationale: write tests mutate data and the parity diff runs twice, so it needs isolation + reset-between-runs + determinism that live production cannot give. Production with sefy@olympus-digital.com is permitted ONLY for read-only GET characterization snapshots, never for write endpoints or parity. Smallest experiment = create a test branch from develop, seed via scripts/seed-yc-demo, snapshot it for reset. NOTE: branch creation requires user approval per supa-project rule.

Non-deterministic responses (timestamps, generated IDs, AI output): the parity differ needs a normalization/ignore list per route. Smallest experiment = run the differ once on an unchanged ref-vs-itself to surface noisy fields. Risk if skipped: false-positive diffs block PRs.

SSE/streaming endpoints (chat): byte-identical diffing does not apply to live streams. Smallest experiment = capture final assembled message + event sequence shape instead of raw stream. Risk if skipped: chat refactors have no usable parity check.
